We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
.
Let
\mathpzc{Sim}
be a QPT algorithm.

1.

The challenger runs
(\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q})
and sends
\mathsf{MPK}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
is allowed to make arbitrary key queries.
For the
\ell
-th key query, the challenger receives
f_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},f_{\ell})
,
and sends
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.
Let
q
be the total number of times that
\mathpzc{A}
makes key queries.
Let
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\{y_{i}\coloneqq f_{i}(m),f_{i},\mathsf{sk}_{f_{i}}\}_{i\in[q]}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The experiment works as follows:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{MPK},m)
, and sends
\mathpzc{ct}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger computes
\mathpzc{ct}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
, and sends
\mathpzc{ct}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
. The output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.

We say that
\Sigma
is
q
-bounded non-adaptive secure if there exists a
QPT
simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}
such that for any
QPT
adversary
\mathpzc{A}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{non}{ada}{sim}(\secp,0)=1] - \Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{non}{ada}{sim}(\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(104)

Definition 4.6 (
q
-Bounded Adaptive Simulation-Security for FE with Certified Everlasting Deletion
[
GVW12
]
).

Let
q
be a polynomial of
\lambda
.
Let
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a
q
-bounded FE with certified everlasting deletion scheme.
We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
.
Let
\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
and
\mathpzc{Sim}_{2}
be a QPT algorithm.

1.

The challenger runs
(\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q})
and sends
\mathsf{MPK}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
is allowed to make arbitrary key queries.
For the
\ell
-th key query, the challenger receives
f_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},f_{\ell})
,
and sends
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.
Let
q_{\mathsf{pre}}
be the number of times that
\mathpzc{A}
makes key queries in this phase.
Let
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\{y_{i}\coloneqq f_{i}(m),f_{i},\mathsf{sk}_{f_{i}}\}_{i\in[q_{\mathsf{pre}}]}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The experiment works as follows:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{MPK},m)
, and sends
\mathpzc{ct}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger computes
(\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{st}_{q_{\mathsf{pre}}})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
, and sends
\mathpzc{ct}
to
\mathpzc{A}
, where
\mathsf{st}_{q_{\mathsf{pre}}}
is a quantum state.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
is allowed to make arbitrary key queries at most
(q-q_{\mathsf{pre}})
times.
For the
\ell
-th key query, the challenger works as follows:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger receives
f_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},f_{\ell})
,
and sends
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger receives
f_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}
, computes
(\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}},\mathsf{st}_{\ell})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{MSK},f_{\ell},f_{\ell}(m),\mathsf{st}_{\ell-1})
, and sends
\mathsf{sk}_{f_{\ell}}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
. The output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.

We say that
\Sigma
is
q
-bounded adaptive simulation-secure if there exists a
QPT
simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}=(\mathpzc{Sim}_{1},\mathpzc{Sim}_{2})
such that for any
QPT
adversary
\mathpzc{A}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{ada}{sim}(\secp,0)=1] - \Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{ada}{sim}(\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(105)

4.2
1
-Bounded Construction with Non-Adaptive Security

To achieve
q
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting simulation-secure FE in
Section

4.4
, we prepare building blocks in this section and
Section

4.3
.
In this section, we construct a
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-secure FE scheme from a certified everlasting secure garbling scheme (
Definition

E.1
) and a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme (
Definition

C.8
). See
Sections

E.2
,
C.4
and
C.5
for how to achieve these building blocks. Regarding PKE, we can also use the construction by Bartusek and Khurana
[
BK23
]
.

Our
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme.

This construction is essentially the same as the
1
-bound FE by Sahai and Seyalioglu
[
SS10
]
. We use a universal circuit
U(\cdot,x)
in which a plaintext
x
is hard-wired. The universal circuit takes a function
f
as input and outputs
f(x)
.
Let
s\coloneqq|f|
.
We construct a
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a certified everlasting secure garbling scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}=\mathsf{GC}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{Garble},\mathpzc{Eval},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
(
Definition

E.1
)
and a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}=\mathsf{PKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
(
Definition

C.8
).

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{MPK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},f)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
f=(f_{1},\cdots,f_{s})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{f}\coloneqq(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{MPK},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Compute
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Compute
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

•

For every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, compute
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},L_{i,\alpha})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})

and
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}_{f}=(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i}\}_{i\in[s]})
and
\mathpzc{ct}=(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

•

For every
i\in[s]
, compute
L_{i},\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]})
.

•

Compute
y\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Eval}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{L_{i}\}_{i\in[s]})
.

•

Output
y
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Del}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}})
.

•

For every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, compute
\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct}_{i,\alpha})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
and
\mathsf{cert}=(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.
Otherwise, output
\bot
.

Correctness:

Correctness easily follows from that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem 4.7.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
satisfies the selective security (
Definition

E.4
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
satisfies the IND-CPA security (
Definition

C.12
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded non-adaptive simulation-security (
Definition

4.5
).

Its proof is similar to that of
Theorem

4.8
, and therefore we omit it.

Theorem 4.8.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
satisfies the selective certified everlasting security (
Definition

E.5
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security (
Definition

C.13
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security (
Definition

4.4
).

Proof of
Theorem

4.8
.

Let us describe how the simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}
works.

\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathcal{V}=\{f(m),f,(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})\}\,\,or\,\,\emptyset
.

2.

If
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
, generate
f\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{s}
.

3.

Generate
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
L_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]
.

4.

Compute
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
.

5.

Compute
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]},L_{i,f[i]})
and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

6.

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
and
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends

\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
If a key query is called,
the challenger receives an function
f
from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
, and sends
(f,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
,
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\allowbreak\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
, and

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},L_{i,\alpha})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
,
and sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger,
and sends its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

If
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert})
, and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

7.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
. If the challenger outputs
\top
, the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except for the following four points.
First, the challenger generates
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
if a key query is not called in step
2
.
Second, the challenger randomly generates
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every
i\in[s]

and
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
in step
2
regardless of whether a key query is called or not.
Third, the challenger does not compute
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
in step
4
.
Fourth,
the challenger computes

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
instead of computing

(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except for the following point.
The challenger computes
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},\allowbreak U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})

instead of computing
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},U(\cdot,m),\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
.

From the definition of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,b)
and
\mathpzc{Sim}
,
it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{non}\mbox{-}\mathsf{adapt}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Therefore,
Theorem

4.8
easily follows from the following
Propositions

4.9
and
4.10
. (whose proof is given later.)
∎

Proposition 4.9.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(106)

Proposition 4.10.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
satisfies the certified everlasting selective security,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(107)

Proof of

4.9
.

For the proof, we use

whose statement and proof is given in

.
We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the security experiment of

.
This contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from

.

\mathpzc{B}

,

to

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.

\mathpzc{B}

from

.

\mathpzc{B}
sends

\mathpzc{B}

m\in\mathcal{M}

and

for every
i\in[s]
, and sends

.

from the challenger. If

from the challenger, it outputs

and aborts.

\mathpzc{B}

outputs

.

.

outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]
and

\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\allowbreak\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]- \Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from

D.9
.

Proof of
Proposition

.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the selective certified everlasting security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathpzc{B}
works below.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every

and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends

\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to

.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
If a key query is called,
\mathpzc{B}
receives an function
f
from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
, generates
L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
for every

, and sends

and

for every

.

, and sends

to

and an input

.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and does the following:

,
and sends

to
\mathpzc{B}
.

•

If

(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{GC}.\mathpzc{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|f|},U(f,m),\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
,
and sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{L_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]},L_{i,f[i]})
and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},L^{*}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{U}},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger, and sends its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{gc}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives

or

from the challenger.
If

receives
\bot
from the challenger, it outputs

and aborts.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}

outputs

.

if all results are

.

It is clear that

and

4.3
1
-Bounded Construction with Adaptive Security

In this section, we convert the non-adaptive scheme constructed in the previous subsection to the adaptive one by using
a certified everlasting secure RNC scheme (
Definition

D.1
).
See
Section

D.2
for how to achieve this building block.

Our
1
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme.

We construct a
1
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}=\mathsf{NAD}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
,
where the ciphertext space is
\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes n}
,
and a certified everlasting secure RNCE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}=\mathsf{NCE}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Fake},\mathsf{Reveal},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
(
Definition

D.1
).
Let
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}
be a QPT algorithm such that

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})\neq\top\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)\\
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}\\
\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Del}(Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c})\\
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

for any
m
.

Our construction is as follows.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Run
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Run
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{MPK}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},f)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{f}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{MPK},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
.

•

Generate
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
. Let
\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
.

•

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},a,c)
and
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}_{f}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
and
\mathpzc{ct}=(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Compute
(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}^{\prime}\coloneqq X^{a^{\prime}}Z^{c^{\prime}}\Psi Z^{c^{\prime}}X^{a^{\prime}}
.

•

Compute
y\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}^{\prime})
.

•

Output
y
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Del}(\Psi)
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},a,c)
and
\mathsf{cert}=(\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}})
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})
.
Otherwise, output
\bot
.

Correctness:

Correctness easily follows from that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem 4.11.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded non-adaptive simulation-security (
Definition

4.5
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
satisfies the RNC security (
Definition

D.3
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded adaptive simulation-security (
Definition

4.6
).

Its proof is similar to that of
Theorem

4.12
, and therefore we omit it.

Theorem 4.12.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security(
Definition

4.4
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
satisfies the certified everlasting RNC security (
Definition

D.4
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security (
Definition

4.2
).

Proof of
Theorem

4.12
.

For a given
2n
-qubit, let
A
be the
n
-qubit of the first half of the
2n
-qubit, and let
B
be the
n
-qubit of the second half of the
2n
-qubit.
Let
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Sim}
be the simulating algorithm of the ciphertext
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
.
Let us describe how the simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}=(\mathpzc{Sim}_{1},\mathpzc{Sim}_{2},\mathpzc{Sim}_{3})
works below.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))\,\,or\,\,\emptyset
.

19
19

19

If an adversary calls a key query before the adversary receives a challenge ciphertext,
then
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
.

2.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
does the following:

•

If
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
, generate
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and

\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.
Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}})
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

•

If
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}))
, generate
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}),1^{|m|})
and
\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
.
Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},a,c,1)
.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{MSK},f,f(m),\mathsf{st})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
and
\mathsf{st}=(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

2.

Compute
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
.

3.

Compute
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}),1^{|m|})
.
Measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[N]
.

4.

Compute
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
.

5.

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{f}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}})
and
\mathsf{st}^{\prime}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x,z,1)
.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{3}(\mathsf{st}^{*})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{st}^{*}=(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x^{*},z^{*},1)
or
\mathsf{st}^{*}=(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\Psi_{B},1^{|m|},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},0)
.

2.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{3}
does the following:

•

If the final bit of
\mathsf{st}^{*}
is
0
,
compute
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\emptyset,1^{|m|})
.
Measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[N]
.
Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x,z)
.

•

If the final bit of
\mathsf{st}^{*}
is
1
, output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},x^{*},z^{*})
.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe},\mathpzc{A}}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(0)
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to make an arbitrary key query at most one time.
For a key query, the challenger receives
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)

and
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and sends
m
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,
computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
,

\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}

and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

If a key query is not called in step
2
,

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to make an arbitrary key query at most one time.
For a key query, the challenger receives
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)

and
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to the challenger and its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

7.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
.
The challenger computes
\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\allowbreak\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})
.
If the results are
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
. The output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
if the challenger outputs
\top
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
in the following second points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and sends
(\Psi,\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}

instead of computing
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))

and sending
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.
Second, in step
5
, the challenger computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(a,c))
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
instead of computing
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
and sending
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
in the following three points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger generates
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
instead of generating
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
and
\Psi=Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and
\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.
Second, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger sends
(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
instead of sending
(\Psi,\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and then that measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis for all
i\in[n]
.
Let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.
Third, the challenger computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
instead of computing
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(a,c))
in step
5
and computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(x,z,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
instead of computing

\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
in step
7
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
:

This is different from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
in the following three points.
First, when a key query is not called in step
2
, the challenger does not generate
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
and measure the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis in step
4
.
Second, if a key query is called in step
5
, the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)

and measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis for all
i\in[n]
after it computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
.
Third, if a key query is not called throughout the experiment,
the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis after step
5
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
except that the challenger computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Sim}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|m|})
instead of computing
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, where
\mathcal{V}=(f,f(m),\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f})
if a key query is called and
\mathcal{V}=\emptyset
if a key query is not called.

From the definition of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,b)
and
\mathpzc{Sim}=(\mathpzc{Sim}_{1},\mathpzc{Sim}_{2},\mathpzc{Sim}_{3})
,
it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Therefore,
Theorem

4.12
easily follows from
Propositions

4.13
,
4.14
,
4.15
and
4.16
.
(Whose proof is given later.)
∎

Proposition 4.13.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
is certified everlasting RNC secure,
it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(113)

Proposition 4.14.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1].

(114)

Proposition 4.15.

\displaystyle\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1].

(115)

Proposition 4.16.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
is
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-secure,
it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{3}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{4}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(116)

Proof of
Proposition

4.13
.

When an adversary makes key queries in step
2
, it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
Hence, we consider the case where the adversary does not make a key query in step
2
below.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the certified everlasting RNC security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathpzc{B}
works below.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,b)
,
generates
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{B}
receives a message
m\in\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},m)
, generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
, computes
\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
, sends
(a,c)
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}^{*},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}^{*})
from the challenger, and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}^{*})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to send a key query at most one time.
For a key query,
\mathpzc{B}
receives an function
f
, generates
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},f)
,
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}^{*})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to
\mathpzc{B}
and its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

5.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK}
or
\bot
from the challenger.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(a,c,\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
and
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*})
.
If the result is
\top
and
\mathpzc{B}
receives
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK}
from the challenger,

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\bot
, sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

7.

\mathpzc{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\allowbreak\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\allowbreak\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}
is non-negligible,
which contradicts the certified everlasting RNC security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.14
.

We clarify the difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
.
First, in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
, the challenger uses
(x,z)
instead of using
(a,c)
as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
.
Second, in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
, the challenger sends
\Psi_{A}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
instead of sending
Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
.
Hence,
it is sufficient to prove that
x
and
z
are uniformly randomly distributed and

\Psi_{A}
is identical to
Z^{z}X^{x}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{x}Z^{z}
.
These two things are obvious from
Lemma

2.2
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.15
.

The difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
is only the order of operating the algorithm
\mathsf{NAD}.\mathpzc{Enc}
and the Bell measurement on
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
.
Therefore, it is clear that the probability distribution of the ciphertext and the decryption key given to the adversary in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
is identical to that the ciphertext and the decryption key given to the adversary in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.16
.

We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{3}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{4}=1]}
is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
.
Let us describe how
\mathpzc{B}
works below.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{noada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,b)
,
generates
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MPK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to call a key query at most one time.
For a key query,
\mathpzc{B}
receives
f
from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
, sends
f
to the challenger as a key query, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}
from the challenger, computes
\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
, and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
and sends
m
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

4.

\mathpzc{B}
does the following.

•

If a key query is called in step
2
,
\mathpzc{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
from the challenger, generates
a,c\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\Psi\coloneqq Z^{c}X^{a}\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}X^{a}Z^{c}
and
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},(a,c))
,
and sends
(\Psi,\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If a key query is not called in step
2
,
\mathpzc{B}
generates
\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}
.
Let
\Psi_{A}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
and
\Psi_{B}\coloneqq\Tr_{A}(\ket{\widetilde{0^n0^n}}\bra{\widetilde{0^n0^n}})
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk})
and sends
(\Psi_{A},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathpzc{ct}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

If a key query is not called in step
2
,

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to make a key query at most one time.
If
\mathpzc{B}
receives an function
f
as key query,
\mathpzc{B}
sends
f
to the challenger as key query, and receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f}
from the challenger.

\mathpzc{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
, measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis, and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{aux},(x,z))
and sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{sk}_{f},\widetilde{\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{sk}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

6.

If
\mathpzc{B}
does not receive a key query throughout the experiment,

\mathpzc{B}
sends a challenge query
m
to the challenger, receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
, and measures the
i
-th qubit of
\mathsf{nad}.\mathpzc{ct}
and
\Psi_{B}
in the Bell basis and let
(x_{i},z_{i})
be the measurement outcome for all
i\in[n]
.

7.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
to
\mathpzc{B}
and its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{NAD}.\mathsf{Recover}(x^{*},z^{*},\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert})
, where
(x^{*},z^{*})=(a,c)
if a key query is called in step
2
and
(x^{*},z^{*})=(x,z)
if a key query is not called in step
2
.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK}
or
\bot
from the challenger.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{NCE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{cert})
.
If the result is
\top
and
\mathpzc{B}
receives
\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK}
from the challenger,
\mathpzc{B}
sends
(\mathsf{nad}.\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{nce}.\mathsf{MSK})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\bot
, sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

9.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

10.

\mathpzc{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{3}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{4}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the
1
-bounded non-adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{nad}}
.
∎

4.4
q
-Bounded Construction with Adaptive Security for
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuits

In this section, we construct a
q
-bounded FE with certified everlasting deletion scheme for all
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuits from
1
-bounded certified everlasting secure FE constructed in the previous subsection
and Shamir’s secret sharing (
[
Sha79
]
).
Our construction is similar to that of standard FE for all
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuits in
[
GVW12
]

except that we use
1
-bounded certified everlasting secure FE instead of standard
1
-bounded FE.

Our
q
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting secure FE scheme for
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuits.

We consider the polynomial representation of circuits
C
in
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
.
The input message space is
\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\mathbb{F}^{\ell}
, and for each
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuit
C
,
C(\cdot)
is an
\ell
-variate polynomial over
\mathbb{F}
of total degree at most
D
.
Let
q=q(\lambda)
be a polynomial of
\lambda
. Our scheme is associated with additional parameters
S=S(\lambda)
,
N=N(\lambda)
,
t=t(\lambda)
and
v=v(\lambda)

that satisfy

\displaystyle t(\lambda)=\Theta(q^{2}\lambda),N(\lambda)=\Theta(D^{2}q^{2}t),v(\lambda)=\Theta(\lambda),S(\lambda)=\Theta(vq^{2}).

(117)

Let us define a family
\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\{G_{C,\Delta}\}_{C\in\mathsf{NC}^{1},\Delta\subseteq[S]}
, where

\displaystyle G_{C,\Delta}(x,Z_{1},Z_{2},\cdots,Z_{S})\coloneqq C(x)+\sum_{i\in\Delta}Z_{i}

(118)

is a function and
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{S}\in\mathbb{F}
.

We construct a
q
-bounded certified everlasting secure FE scheme for all
\mathsf{NC}^{1}
circuits
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\allowbreak\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a
1
-bounded certified everlasting secure FE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}=\mathsf{ONE}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\allowbreak\mathsf{Vrfy})
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

For
i\in[N]
, generate
(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{MPK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK},C)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

•

Chooses a uniformly random set
\Gamma\subseteq[N]
of size
tD+1
.

•

Chooses a uniformly random set
\Delta\subseteq[S]
of size
v
.

•

For
i\in\Gamma
, compute
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C,\Delta,i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},G_{C,\Delta})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}_{C}\coloneqq(\Gamma,\Delta,\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C,\Delta,i}\}_{i\in\Gamma})
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{MPK},x)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

•

For
i\in[\ell]
, pick a random degree
t
polynomial
\mu_{i}(\cdot)
whose constant term is
x[i]
.

•

For
i\in[S]
, pick a random degree
Dt
polynomial
\xi_{i}(\cdot)
whose constant term is
0
.

•

For
i\in[N]
, compute
(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},(\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i),\allowbreak\xi_{1}(i),\cdots,\xi_{S}(i)))
.

•

Output
\mathsf{vk}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk}_{C},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}_{C}=(\Gamma,\Delta,\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C,\Delta,i}\}_{i\in\Gamma})
and
\mathpzc{ct}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

•

For
i\in\Gamma
, compute
\eta(i)\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C,\Delta,i},\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i})
.

•

Output
\eta(0)
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

•

For
i\in[N]
, compute
\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{cert}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathsf{cert}=\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.

•

For
i\in[N]
, compute
\top/\bot\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{cert}_{i})
.
If all results are
\top
, output
\top
. Otherwise, output
\bot
.

Correctness:

Verification correctness easily follows from verification correctness of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
.
Let us show evaluation correctness.
By decryption correctness of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
, for all
i\in\Gamma
we have

\displaystyle\eta(i)

\displaystyle=G_{C,\Delta}(\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i),\xi_{1}(i),\cdots,\xi_{S}(i))

(119)

\displaystyle=C(\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i))+\Sigma_{a\in\Delta}\xi_{a}(i).

(120)

Since
|\Gamma|\geq Dt+1
, this means that
\eta
is equal to the degree
Dt
polynomial

\displaystyle\eta(\cdot)=C(\mu_{1}(\cdot),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(\cdot))+\Sigma_{a\in\Delta}\xi_{a}(\cdot)

(121)

Hence
\eta(0)=C(x_{1},\cdots,x_{\ell})=C(x)
, which means that our construction satisfies evaluation correctness.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem 4.17.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded adaptive simulation-security,
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
satisfies the
q
-bounded adaptive simulation-security.

Its proof is similar to that of
Theorem

4.18
, and therefore we omit it.

Theorem 4.18.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
satisfies the
1
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security,
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}}
the
q
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting simulation-security.

Proof of
Theorem

4.18
.

Let us denote the simulating algorithm of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
as
\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Sim}=\mathsf{ONE}.(\mathpzc{Sim}_{1},\mathpzc{Sim}_{2},\mathpzc{Sim}_{3})
.
Let us describe how the simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}=(\mathpzc{Sim}_{1},\mathpzc{Sim}_{2},\mathpzc{Sim}_{3})
works below.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{MPK},\mathcal{V},1^{|x|})
:

Let
q^{*}
be the number of times that
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
has made key queries before it sends a challenge query.

1.

Parse
\mathsf{MPK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\{C_{j},C_{j}(x),(\Gamma_{j},\Delta_{j},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}\}_{i\in[\Gamma_{j}]})\}_{j\in[q^{*}]}
.

2.

Generate a uniformly random set
\Gamma_{i}\subseteq[N]
of size
Dt+1
and a uniformly random set
\Delta_{i}\subseteq[S]
of size
v
for all
i\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\}
.
Let
\Delta_{0}\coloneqq\emptyset
. Let
\mathcal{L}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\neq i^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i^{\prime}})
.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
aborts if
\left|\mathcal{L}\right|>t
or
there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\setminus(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.

3.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
uniformly and independently samples
\ell
random degree
t
polynomials
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{\ell}
whose constant terms are all
0
.

4.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
samples the polynomials
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{S}
as follows for
j\in[q]
:

•

fix
a^{*}\in\Delta_{j}\setminus(\Delta_{0}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{j-1})
;

•

for all
a\in(\Delta_{j}\setminus(\Delta_{0}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{j-1}))\setminus\{a^{*}\}
, set
\xi_{a}
to be a uniformly random degree
Dt
polynomial whose constant term is
0
;

•

if
j\leq q^{*}
, pick a random degree
Dt
polynomial
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
whose constant term is
C_{j}(x)
; if
j>q^{*}
, pick random values for
\eta_{j}(i)
for all
i\in\mathcal{L}
;

•

the evaluation of
\xi_{a^{*}}
on the points in
\mathcal{L}
is defined by the relation:

\displaystyle\eta_{j}(\cdot)=C_{j}(\mu_{1}(\cdot),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(\cdot))+\sum_{a\in\Delta_{j}}\xi_{a}(\cdot).

(122)

•

Finally, for all
a\notin(\Delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\Delta_{q})
, set
\xi_{a}
to be a uniformly random degree
Dt
polynomial whose constant term is
0
.

5.

For each
i\in\mathcal{L}
,
\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},(\mu_{1}(i),\cdots,\mu_{\ell}(i),\xi_{1}(i),\cdots,\xi_{S}(i))).

(123)

6.

For each
i\notin\mathcal{L}
,
\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}
does the following:

•

If
i\in\Gamma_{j}
for some
j\in[q^{*}]

20
20

20

Note that
j
is uniquely determined since
i\notin\mathcal{L}
.

,
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},(G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i},\eta_{j}(i),\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i}),1^{|m|}).

(124)

•

If
i\notin\Gamma_{j}
for all
j\in[q^{*}]
,
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Sim}_{1}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},\emptyset,1^{|m|}).

(125)

7.

Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
and
\mathsf{st}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{j}(i)\}_{j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})
.

\mathpzc{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{MSK},C_{j},C_{j}(x),\mathsf{st})
:

The simulator simulates the
j
-th key query for

.

1.

Parse

.

For each

For each
i\in\Gamma_{j}\setminus\mathcal{L}
,

Dt
polynomial
\eta_{j}(\cdot)

and subject to the constraints on the values in

chosen earlier,

\displaystyle(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{sk}_{C_{j},\Delta_{j},i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}^{*})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathpzc{Sim}_{2}(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i},\eta_{j}(i),G_{C_{j},\Delta_{j}},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}).

For simplicity, let us denote

Output

.

:

The simulator simulates a verification key.

1.

Parse
\mathsf{st}^{*}\coloneqq(\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\eta_{j}(i)\}_{j\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\},i\in\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{st}_{i}\}_{i\in[N]\setminus\mathcal{L}},\{\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{L}})

Output

.

Let us define the sequence of hybrids as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,0)
.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MPK}_{i},\mathsf{one}.\mathsf{MSK}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{ONE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})

.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is allowed to call key queries at most
q
times.
For the

from

, generates a uniformly random set

and

of size

.
For

has called key queries in this step.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses

polynomial

.

times.
For the

from

, generates a uniformly random set

If

outputs

.

The experiment outputs

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except for the following three points.
First, the challenger generates uniformly random set

.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}

From the definition of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,b)
and

,
it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cefe}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sim}}(\lambda,1)=1]

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Proposition 4.20.

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}=1].

Proposition 4.21.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{one}}
is
1
-bounded adaptive certified everlasting simulation-secure,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{3}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}=1].

Proof of
Proposition

4.19
.

Let
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\prime}
be the experiment identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except that the challenger generates a set
\Gamma_{i}\in[N]
and
\Delta_{i}\in[S]
for
i\in\{q^{*}+1,\cdots,q\}

in step
4
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\prime}=1]
.

Let
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}
be the experiment identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\prime}
except that it outputs
\bot
if
|\mathcal{L}|>t
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\prime}=1\wedge(|\mathcal{L}|\leq t)]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}=1\wedge(|\mathcal{L}|\leq t)]
.
Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybij{0}{\prime}=1]-\Pr[\hybij{0}{*}=1]}\leq\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]

(128)

from
Lemma

2.3
.

Let
\mathsf{Collide}
be the event that
there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\setminus(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}
is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
when
\mathsf{Collide}
does not occur.
Hence, it is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{*}=1\wedge\overline{\mathsf{Collide}}]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1\wedge\overline{\mathsf{Collide}}]
.
Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybij{0}{*}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}]

(129)

from
Lemma

2.3
.

From the discussion above, we have

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]+\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}].

(130)

The following
Lemmas

4.23
and
4.24
shows that

\Pr[|\mathcal{L}|>t]\leq 2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

and
\Pr[\mathsf{Collide}]\leq q2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.23 (
[
GVW12
]
).

Let
\Gamma_{1},\cdots,\Gamma_{q}\subseteq[N]
be randomly chosen subsets of size
tD+1
.
Let
t=\Theta(q^{2}\lambda)
and
N=\Theta(D^{2}q^{2}t)
.
Then,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left|\bigcup_{i\neq i^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{i}\cap\Gamma_{i})\right|>t\right]\leq 2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

(131)

where the probability is over the random choice of the subsets
\Gamma_{1},\cdots,\Gamma_{q}
.

Lemma 4.24 (
[
GVW12
]
).

Let
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{q}\subseteq[S]
be randomly chosen subsets of size
v
.
Let
v(\lambda)=\Theta(\lambda)
and
S(\lambda)=\Theta(vq^{2})
.
Let
\mathsf{Collide}
be the event that there exists some
i\in[q]
such that
\Delta_{i}\backslash(\bigcup_{j\neq i}\Delta_{j})=\emptyset
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Collide}\right]\leq q2^{-\Omega(\lambda)}

(132)

where the probability is over the random choice of subsets
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{q}
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.20
.

In the encryption in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
,
\xi_{a^{*}}
is chosen at random and
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
is defined by the relation.

\mathpzc{Sim}
essentially chooses
\eta_{j}(\cdot)
at random which defines
\xi_{a^{*}}
.
It is easy to see that reversing the order of how the polynomials are chosen produces the same distribution.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.21
.

To prove the proposition, let us define a hybrid experiment
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}{s}
for each
s\in[N]
as follows.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
except for the following three points.
First, the challenger generates
\{\mathsf{one}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i}\}_{i\in[s]\setminus\mathcal{L}}

,

Third, the challenger generates

\mathpzc{Sim}_{3}
.

Let us denote
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{0}
.
It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{N}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]
.
Furthermore, we can show that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybij{2}{s-1}=1]-\Pr[\hybij{2}{s}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

for

Let us show the remaining one.

.

Then, we can construct an adversary

For the

.

outputs

.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s-1}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{s}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybij{2}{s-1}=1]-\Pr[\hybij{2}{s}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore
