(244)

C.2
SKE Scheme with QROM

In this section, we construct an SKE with certified everlasting deletion scheme with QROM.
Our construction is similar to that of the certified everlasting commitment scheme in
[
HMNY22b
]
.
The difference is that we use SKE instead of commitment.

Our certified everlasting secure SKE scheme.

We construct a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}=(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
from the following primitives.

•

A one-time SKE with certified deletion scheme (
Definition

2.19
)
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}=\mathsf{CD}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
.

•

A SKE scheme (
Definition

2.8
)
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}=\mathsf{SKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
with plaintext space
\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

A hash function
H
modeled as a quantum random oracle.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}\coloneqq\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
and
R\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},R)
.

•

Compute
h\coloneqq H(R)\oplus\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk},m)
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct})
and
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathpzc{ct}=(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Compute
R^{\prime}{\bf\,\,or\,\,}\bot\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct})
.
If it outputs
\bot
,
\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ct})
outputs
\bot
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}\coloneqq H(R^{\prime})\oplus h
.

•

Compute
m^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime},\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Output
m^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{cert}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathsf{cert}=\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{cert}
.

•

Compute
b\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{cert})
.

•

Output
b
.

Correctness:

It is easy to see that correctness of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
comes from those of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
.
Special correctness holds due to that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
. Verifcation correctness with QOTP holds due to that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem C.14.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
satisfies the OW-CPA security (
Definition

2.11
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
satisfies the OT-CD security (
Definition

2.19
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
satisfies the IND-CPA security (
Definition

C.6
).

Its proof is similar to that of
Theorem

C.15
, and therefore we omit it.

Theorem C.15.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
satisfies the OW-CPA security (
Definition

2.11
)
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
satisfies the OT-CD security (
Definition

2.19
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security (
Definition

C.7
).

Its proof is similar to that of
[
HMNY22b
, Theorem 5.8]
.

C.3
SKE Scheme without QROM

In this section, we construct an SKE with certified everlasting deletion scheme without QROM.
Note that unlike the construction with QROM (
Section

C.2
), in this construction
the plaintext space is of constant size.
However, the size can be extended to the polynomial size via the standard hybrid argument.
Our construction is similar to that of revocable quantum timed-release encryption in
[
Unr15
]
.
The difference is that we use SKE instead of timed-release encryption.

Our certified everlasting secure SKE scheme without QROM.

Let
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
be constants such that
k_{1}>k_{2}
.
Let
p
,
q
,
r
,
s
and
t
be polynomials.
Let
(C_{1},C_{2})
be a CSS code with parameters
q,k_{1},k_{2},t
.
We construct a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}=(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
with plaintext space
\mathcal{M}=C_{1}/C_{2}
(isomorphic to
\{0,1\}^{k_{1}-k_{2}}
),
ciphertext space
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes\left(p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)\right)}\times\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{q(\lambda)}/C_{1}\times C_{1}/C_{2}
,
secret key space
\mathcal{SK}=\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
,
verification key space
\mathcal{VK}=\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times[p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)]_{p(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}

and deletion certificate space
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes\left(p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)\right)}

from the following primitive.

•

An SKE scheme (
Definition

2.8
)
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}=\mathsf{SKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
with plaintext space
\mathcal{M}=\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times[p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)]_{p(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times C_{1}/C_{2}
, secret key space
\mathcal{SK}=\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
and ciphertext space
\mathcal{C}=\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}
.

The construction is as follows. (We will omit the security parameter below.)

\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}\coloneqq\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
.

•

Generate
B\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{p}
,
Q\leftarrow[p+q]_{p}
,
y\leftarrow C_{1}/C_{2}
,
u\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{q}/C_{1}
,
r\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{p}
,

x\leftarrow C_{1}/C_{2}
,
w\leftarrow C_{2}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Enc}\left(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},(B,Q,r,y)\right)
.

•

Let
U_{Q}
be the unitary that permutes the qubits in
Q
into the first half of the system.
(I.e.,
U_{Q}\ket{x_1x_2\cdots x_{p+q}}=\ket{x_{a_1}x_{a_2}\cdots x_{a_p}x_{b_1}x_{b_2}\cdots x_{b_q}}
with
Q\coloneqq\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\}
and
\{1,2,\cdots,p+q\}\backslash Q\coloneqq\{b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\}
.)

•

Construct a quantum state
\ket{\Psi}\coloneqq U_{Q}^{\dagger}(H^{B}\otimes I^{\otimes q})(\ket{r}\otimes\ket{x\oplus w\oplus u})
.

•

Compute
h\coloneqq m\oplus x\oplus y
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
and
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(B,Q,r)
.

\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
,
\mathpzc{ct}=(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
.

•

Compute
(B,Q,r,y)/\bot\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct})
.
If
\bot\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct})
,
\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ct})
outputs
\bot
and aborts.

•

Apply
U_{Q}
to
\ket{\Psi}
, measure the last
q
-qubits in the computational basis and obtain the measurement outcome
\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{q}
.

•

Compute
x\coloneqq\gamma\oplus u
mod
C_{2}
.

•

Output
m^{\prime}\coloneqq h\oplus x\oplus y
.

\mathsf{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{cert}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi}
.

\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(B,Q,r)
and
\mathpzc{cert}=\ket{\Psi}
.

•

Apply
(H^{B}\otimes I^{\otimes q})U_{Q}
to
\ket{\Psi}
, measure the first
p
-qubits in the computational basis and obtain the measurement outcome
r^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{p}
.

•

Output
\top
if
r=r^{\prime}
and output
\bot
otherwise.

Correctness.

Correctness easily follows from that of

\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
.
Special correctness holds due to that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
. Verifcation correctness with QOTP holds since
\mathsf{Recover}
is the decryption algorithm of QOTP.

Security.

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem C.16.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
is IND-CPA secure (
Definition

2.12
), then
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
is IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.6
).

Its proof is straightforward, so we omit it.

Theorem C.17.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{sk}}
is IND-CPA secure (
Definition

2.12
) and
tp/(p+q)-4(k_{1}-k_{2}){\rm ln}2
is superlogarithmic, then
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.7
).

Its proof is similar to that of
[
Unr15
, Theorem 3]
.

Note that the plaintext space is of constant size in our construction. However,
via the standard hybrid argument , we can extend it to the polynomial size.

C.4
PKE Scheme with QROM

In this section, we construct a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme with QROM.
Our construction is similar to that of the certified everlasting commitment scheme in
[
HMNY22b
]
.
The difference is that we use PKE instead of commitment.

Our certified everlasting secure PKE scheme.

We construct a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}=(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a one-time SKE with certified deletion scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}=\mathsf{SKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
(
Definition

2.19
),
a PKE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}=\mathsf{PKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
with plaintext space
\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
(
Definition

2.15
)
and a hash function
H
modeled as quantum random oracle.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{pk}\coloneqq\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}
and
\mathsf{sk}\coloneqq\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Randomly generate
R\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk},R)
.

•

Compute
h\coloneqq H(R)\oplus\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},m)
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct})
and
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathpzc{ct}=(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct})
.

•

Compute
R^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}\coloneqq h\oplus H(R^{\prime})
.

•

Compute
m^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Output
m^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(h,\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct})
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathsf{cert}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}
.

•

Compute
b\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert})
.

•

Output
b
.

Correctness:

Correctness easily follows from those of

\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}
and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
.
Verifcation correctness with QOTP holds due to that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.
Their proofs are similar to those of
Theorems

C.14
and
C.15
, and therefore we omit them.

Theorem C.18.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}
satisfies the OW-CPA security (
Definition

2.17
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
satisfies the OT-CD security (
Definition

2.22
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
is IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.12
).

Theorem C.19.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}
satisfies the OW-CPA security (
Definition

2.17
) and
\Sigma_{\mathsf{skcd}}
satisfies the OT-CD security (
Definition

2.22
),

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.13
).

C.5
PKE Scheme without QROM

In this section, we construct a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme
without QROM.
Our construction is similar to that of quantum timed-release encryption presented in
[
Unr15
]
.
The difference is that we use PKE instead of timed-release encryption.

Our certified everlasting secure PKE scheme without QROM.

Let
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
be some constant such that
k_{1}>k_{2}
.
Let
p
,
q
,
r
,
s
,
t
and
u
be some polynomials.
Let
(C_{1},C_{2})
be a CSS code with parameters
q,k_{1},k_{2},t
.
We construct a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}=(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
, with plaintext space
\mathcal{M}=C_{1}/C_{2}
(isomorphic
\{0,1\}^{(k_{1}-k_{2})}
),
ciphertext space
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes\left(p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)\right)}\times\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{q(\lambda)}/C_{1}\times C_{1}/C_{2}
,
public key space
\mathcal{PK}=\{0,1\}^{u(\lambda)}
,
secret key space
\mathcal{SK}=\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
,
verification key space
\mathcal{VK}=\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times[p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)]_{p(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}

and deletion certificate space
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes\left(p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)\right)}

from the following primitive.

•

A PKE scheme (
Definition

2.15
)
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}=\mathsf{PKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
with
plaintext space
\mathcal{M}=\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times[p(\lambda)+q(\lambda)]_{p(\lambda)}\times\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}\times C_{1}/C_{2}
,
public key space
\mathcal{PK}=\{0,1\}^{u(\lambda)}
,
secret key space
\mathcal{SK}=\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
and ciphertext space
\mathcal{C}=\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}
.

The construction is as follows. (We will omit the security parameter below.)

\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{pk}\coloneqq\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}
and
\mathsf{sk}\coloneqq\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}
.

•

Generate
B\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{p}
,
Q\leftarrow[p+q]_{p}
,
y\leftarrow C_{1}/C_{2}
,
u\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{q}/C_{1}
,
r\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{p}
,

x\leftarrow C_{1}/C_{2}
,
w\leftarrow C_{2}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}\left(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk},(B,Q,r,y)\right)
.

•

Let
U_{Q}
be the unitary that permutes the qubits in
Q
into the first half of the system.
(I.e.,
U_{Q}\ket{x_1x_2\cdots x_{p+q}}=\ket{x_{a_1}x_{a_2}\cdots x_{a_p}x_{b_1}x_{b_2}\cdots x_{b_q}}
with
Q\coloneqq\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{p}\}
and
\{1,2,\cdots,p+q\}\setminus Q\coloneqq\{b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{q}\}
.)

•

Generate a quantum state
\ket{\Psi}\coloneqq U_{Q}^{\dagger}(H^{B}\otimes I^{\otimes q})(\ket{r}\otimes\ket{x\oplus w\oplus u})
.

•

Compute
h\coloneqq m\oplus x\oplus y
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
and
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(B,Q,r)
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathpzc{ct}=(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
.

•

Compute
(B,Q,r,y)\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct})
.

•

Apply
U_{Q}
to
\ket{\Psi}
, measure the last
q
-qubits in the computational basis and obtain the measurement outcome
\gamma
.

•

Compute
x\coloneqq\gamma\oplus u
mod
C_{2}
.

•

Output
m^{\prime}\coloneqq h\oplus x\oplus y
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=(\ket{\Psi},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{ct},u,h)
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{cert}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi}
.

\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(B,Q,r)
and
\mathpzc{cert}=\ket{\Psi}
.

•

Apply
(H^{B}\otimes I^{\otimes q})U_{Q}
to
\ket{\Psi}
, measure the first
p
-qubits in the computational basis and obtain the measurement outcome
r^{\prime}
.

•

Output
\top
if
r=r^{\prime}
and output
\bot
otherwise.

Correctness easily follows from that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}
.
Verifcation correctness with QOTP holds since
\mathsf{Recover}
is the decryption algorithm of QOTP.

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem C.20.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{pk}}

), then

).

is superlogarithmic,
then

C.13
).

[
Unr15
, Theorem 3]
.

Appendix D
Receiver Non-Committing Encryption with Certified Everlasting Deletion

In this section, we define and construct receiver non-committing encryption with certified everlasting deletion.
In
Section

D.1
, we define RNCE with certified everlasting deletion.
In
Section

D.2
, we construct a certified everlasting RNCE scheme from certified everlasting secure PKE (
Appendix

C
).

D.1
Definition

Definition D.1 (RNCE with Certified Everlasting Deletion (Syntax)).

Let
\lambda
be the security parameter and let
p
,
q
,
r
,
s
,
t
,
u
, and
v
be polynomials.
An RNCE with certified everlasting deletion scheme is a tuple of algorithms
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Fake},\mathsf{Reveal},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

with plaintext space
\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{n}
,
ciphertext space
\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes p(\lambda)}
,
public key space
\mathcal{PK}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{q(\lambda)}
,
master secret key space
\mathcal{MSK}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}
,
secret key space
\mathcal{SK}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
,
verification key space
\mathcal{VK}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{t(\lambda)}
,
deletion certificate space
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{u(\lambda)}
,
and auxiliary state space
\mathcal{AUX}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{v(\lambda)}
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK})
:

The setup algorithm takes the security parameter
1^{\lambda}
as input, and outputs a public key
\mathsf{pk}\in\mathcal{PK}
and a master secret key
\mathsf{MSK}\in\mathcal{MSK}
.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK})\rightarrow\mathsf{sk}
:

The key generation algorithm takes the master secret key
\mathsf{MSK}
as input,
and outputs a secret key
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{SK}
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)\rightarrow(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

The encryption algorithm takes
\mathsf{pk}
and a plaintext
m\in\mathcal{M}
as input,
and outputs a verification key
\mathsf{vk}\in\mathcal{VK}
and a ciphertext
\mathpzc{ct}\in\mathcal{C}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})\rightarrow m^{\prime}~or~\bot
:

The decryption algorithm takes
\mathsf{sk}
and
\mathsf{ct}
as input, and outputs a plaintext
m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}
or
\bot
.

\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{pk})\rightarrow(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{aux})
:

The fake encryption algorithm takes
\mathsf{pk}
as input, and outputs a verification key
\mathsf{vk}\in\mathcal{VK}
, a fake ciphertext
\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}}\in\mathcal{C}
and an auxiliary state
\mathsf{aux}\in\mathcal{AUX}
.

\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{aux},m)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}}
:

The reveal algorithm takes
\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{aux}
and
m
as input, and outputs a fake secret key
\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}}\in\mathcal{SK}
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})\rightarrow\mathsf{cert}
:

The deletion algorithm takes
\mathpzc{ct}
as input and outputs a certification
\mathsf{cert}\in\mathcal{D}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})\rightarrow\top
or
\bot
:

The verification algorithm takes
\mathsf{vk}
and
\mathsf{cert}
as input, and outputs
\top
or
\bot
.

We require that an RNCE with certified everlasting deletion scheme satisfies correctness defined below.

Definition D.2 (Correctness for RNCE with Certified Everlasting Deletion).

There are two types of correctness, namely, decryption correctness and verification correctness.

Decryption Correctness:

There exists a negligible function
{\mathsf{negl}}
such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[m^{\prime}\neq m\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)\\
\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK})\\
m^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification Correctness:

There exists a negligible function
{\mathsf{negl}}
such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})=\bot\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)\\
\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

As security, we consider two definitions,

Definition

D.3

and

Definition

D.4
given below.
The former is just the standard receiver non-committing security
and the latter is the certified everlasting security that we newly define in this paper.
Roughly, the everlasting security guarantees that any QPT adversary cannot distinguish whether the ciphertext and the secret key are properly generated or not even if it becomes computationally unbounded and obtains the master secret key after it issues a valid certificate.

Definition D.3 (Receiver Non-Committing Security for RNCE with Certified Everlasting Deletion).

Let
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Fake},\mathsf{Reveal},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be an RNCE with certified everlasting deletion scheme.
We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
.

1.

The challenger runs
(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and sends
\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
sends
m\in\mathcal{M}
to the challenger.

3.

The challenger does the following:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger generates
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)
and
\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK})
, and sends
(\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger generates
(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{pk})
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{aux},m)
, and sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

We say that
\Sigma
is receiver non-committing (RNC) secure if, for any QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{rec}{nc}(\secp,0)=1] - \Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{rec}{nc}(\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(252)

Definition D.4 (Certified Everlasting RNC Security for RNCE).

Let
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Fake},\mathsf{Reveal},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a certified everlasting RNCE scheme.
We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and an unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

1.

The challenger runs
(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and sends
\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
m\in\mathcal{M}
to the challenger.

3.

The challenger does the following:

•

If
b=0
, the challenger generates
(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{ct})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)
and
\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK})
, and sends
(\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{sk})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If
b=1
, the challenger generates
(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{aux})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{pk})
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{aux},m)
,
and sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger and its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

5.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
.
If the output is
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
and sends
\mathsf{MSK}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
If the output is
\bot
, the challenger outputs
\bot
and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

7.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

We say that
\Sigma
is certified everlasting RNC secure if for any QPT
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
,
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expd{\Sigma,\qA}{cert}{ever}{rec}{nc}(\secp,0)=1] - \Pr[\expd{\Sigma,\qA}{cert}{ever}{rec}{nc}(\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(253)

D.2
Construction

In this section, we construct a certified everlasting RNCE scheme from a certified everlasting PKE scheme (
Definition

C.8
).
Our construction is similar to that of the secret-key RNCE scheme presented in
[
KNTY19
]
.
The difference is that we use a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme instead of a standard SKE scheme.

Our certified everlasting secure RNCE scheme.

We construct a certified everlasting secure RNCE scheme

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Fake},\mathsf{Reveal},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}=\mathsf{PKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
, which was introduced in
Definition

C.8
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{pk}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathsf{MSK}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

\mathsf{KeyGen}(\mathsf{MSK})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{MSK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Generate
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sk}\coloneqq(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]})
.

\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},m[i])
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathpzc{ct}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{sk}=(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]})
and
\mathpzc{ct}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Compute
m[i]\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]})
for all
i\in[n]
.

•

Output
m\coloneqq m[1]||m[2]||\cdots||m[n]
.

\mathpzc{Fake}(\mathsf{pk})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Generate
x^{*}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x^{*}[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x^{*}[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x^{*}[i]},0)
and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1},\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1},1)
for all
i\in[n]
.

•

Output
\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
,

\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathsf{aux}=x^{*}
.

\mathsf{Reveal}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{MSK},\mathsf{aux},m)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
,
\mathsf{MSK}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathsf{aux}=x^{*}
.

•

Output
\widetilde{\mathsf{sk}}\coloneqq\left(x^{*}\oplus m,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus m[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}\right)
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{ct}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha})
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
and
\mathsf{cert}=\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

Compute
\top/\bot\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha})
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.
If all results are
\top
,
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
outputs
\top
. Otherwise, it outputs
\bot
.

Correctness:

Correctness easily follows from that of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
.

Security:

The following two theorems hold.

Theorem D.5.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
is IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.12
),
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
is RNC secure (
Definition

D.3
).

Its proof is similar to that of
Theorem

D.6
, and therefore we omit it.

Theorem D.6.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure (
Definition

C.13
),
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}}
is certified everlasting RNC secure (
Definition

D.4
).

Proof of
Theorem

D.6
.

To prove the theorem, let us introduce the sequence of hybrids.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cence}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,0)
.
For clarity, we describe the experiment against any adversary
\mathpzc{A}=(\mathpzc{A}_{1},\mathpzc{A}_{2})
, where
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
is any QPT adversary and
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
is any unbounded adversary.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

2.

The challenger sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
m\in\mathcal{M}
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger generates
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
, computes
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\allowbreak\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},m[i])
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to the challenger and its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha})
for all
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.
If all results are
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
and sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
. Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

7.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

8.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except that
the challenger generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\allowbreak\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},\allowbreak m[i]\oplus 1)
for all
i\in[n]
instead of computing
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},m[i])
for all
i\in[n]
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except for the following three points.

1.

The challenger generates
x^{*}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
instead of generating
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
.

2.

For all
i\in[n]
, the challenger generates
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x^{*}[i]},\allowbreak\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x^{*}[i]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x^{*}[i]},0)

and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus 1},1)

instead of computing
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]})\allowbreak\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x[i]},m[i])

and
(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1},m[i]\oplus 1)
.

3.

The challenger sends
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},(x^{*}\oplus m,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus m[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}

instead of sending
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

It is clear that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,0)
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{rec}\mbox{-}\mathsf{nc}}(\lambda,1)
.
Hence,
Theorem

D.6
easily follows from the following
Propositions

D.7
and
D.8

(whose proof is given later.).
∎

Proposition D.7.

If
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure,
it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.

Proposition D.8.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.

Proof of
Proposition

D.7
.

For the proof, we use
Lemma

D.9
.
We assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible,
and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
defined in
Lemma

D.9
.
This contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from
Lemma

D.9
.
Let us describe how
\mathpzc{B}
works below.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
from the challenger of

\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
.

2.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
m\in\mathcal{M}
and sends
m
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

4.

\mathpzc{B}
generates
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
and sends
(x,m[1],\cdots,m[n],m[1]\oplus 1,\cdots,m[n]\oplus 1)
to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
.

5.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[n]})
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
.

6.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]},\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]})\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{pk}_{i,x[i]},m[i])
for
i\in[n]
.

7.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

8.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{B}
and the internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

9.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[n]}
to the challenger, and receives
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[n]}
or
\bot
.
If
\mathpzc{B}
receives
\bot
, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

10.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

11.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

12.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{vk}_{i,x[i]},\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{cert}_{i,x[i]})
for all
i\in[n]
.
If all results are
\top
,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{0}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible.
Therefore,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}
is also non-negligible,
which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cepk}}
from
Lemma

D.9
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

D.8
.

It is obvious that the joint probability distribution that
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
receives
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},\allowbreak(x,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
is identical to the joint probability distribution that
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
receives
(\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathpzc{ct}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}},\allowbreak(x^{*}\oplus m,\{\mathsf{pke}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,x^{*}[i]\oplus m[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}))
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
.
Hence,
Proposition

D.8
follows.
∎

We use the following lemma for the proof of
Theorem

D.6
and
Theorem

E.7
.
The proof is shown with the standard hybrid argument.
It is also easy to see that a similar lemma holds for IND-CPA security.

Lemma D.9.

Let
s
be some polynomial of the security parameter
\lambda
.
Let
\Sigma\coloneqq(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a certified everlasting secure PKE scheme.
Let us consider the following security experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
against
\mathpzc{A}
consisting of any QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

1.

The challenge generates
(\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for all
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
,
and sends
\{\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
and
(m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])\in\mathcal{M}^{2s}
,
and sends
(f,m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\\
\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])
to the challenger.

3.

The challenger computes

(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},m_{b}[i])
for all
i\in[s]
,
and sends
(\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]},\\
\{\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to the challenger,
and sends its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

5.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.
If all results are
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

7.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

If the
\Sigma
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[ \expc{\Sigma,\qA}{multi}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1] - \Pr[ \expc{\Sigma,\qA}{multi}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 1)=1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

(254)

for any QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

Proof of
Lemma

D.9
.

Let us consider the following hybrids for
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,s\}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}
:

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for every
i\in[s]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and sends
\{\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
and
(m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])\in\mathcal{M}^{2s}
,
and sends
(f,m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])
to the challenger.

3.

The challenger computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},m_{1}[i])

(255)

for
i\in[j]
and

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},m_{0}[i])

(256)

for
i\in\{j+1,j+2,\cdots,s\}
,
and sends
(\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]},\{\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to the challenger,
and sends its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

5.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathsf{cert}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})
for every
i\in[s]
.
If all results are
\top
, the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

7.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)=1]
and
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{s}=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{multi}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,1)=1]
.
Furthermore, we can show

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{j}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{j+1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

(257)

for each
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,s-1\}
.
(Its proof is given below.) From these facts, we obtain
Lemma

D.9
.

Let us show the remaining one. To show it, let us assume that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{j}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{j+1}=1]}
is non-negligible.
Then, we can construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that can break the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma
as follows.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
\mathsf{pk}
from the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
.

2.

\mathpzc{B}
generates
\beta\leftarrow\{0,1\}
and sets
\mathsf{pk}_{j+1,\beta}\coloneqq\mathsf{pk}
.

3.

\mathpzc{B}
generates
(\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha},\mathsf{sk}_{i,\alpha})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
for
i\in\{1,\cdots,j,j+2,\cdots,s\}
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, and
(\mathsf{pk}_{j+1,\beta\oplus 1},\mathsf{sk}_{j+1,\beta\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

4.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{pk}_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[s],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
f\in\{0,1\}^{s}
and
(m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])\in\mathcal{M}^{2s}
,
and sends
(f,m_{0}[1],m_{0}[2],\\
\cdots,m_{0}[s],m_{1}[1],m_{1}[2],\cdots,m_{1}[s])
to the challenger.

6.

If
f[j+1]=\beta
,
\mathpzc{B}
aborts the experiment, and outputs
\bot
.

7.

\mathpzc{B}
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},m_{1}[i])

(258)

for
i\in[j]
and

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1},m_{0}[i])

(259)

for
i\in\{j+2,\cdots,s\}
.

8.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(m_{0}[j+1],m_{1}[j+1])
to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
.
The challenger computes
(\mathsf{vk}_{j+1,f[j+1]\oplus 1},\mathpzc{ct}_{j+1,f[j+1]\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{pk}_{j+1,f[j+1]\oplus 1},m_{b}[j+1])
and sends
\mathpzc{ct}_{j+1,f[j+1]\oplus 1}
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

9.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]}\}_{i\in[s]},\{\mathpzc{ct}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

10.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[s]}
to
\mathpzc{B}
, and sends its internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

11.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{cert}_{j+1}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{sk}_{j+1,f[j+1]\oplus 1}
or
\bot
from the challenger.
If
\mathpzc{B}
receives
\bot
from the challenger, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

12.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{sk}_{i,f[i]\oplus 1}\}_{i\in[s]}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

13.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

14.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}
for all
\mathsf{cert}_{i}
, and outputs
b^{\prime}
if all results are
\top
. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\bot
.

Since
\mathsf{pk}_{j+1,\beta}
and
\mathsf{pk}_{j+1,\beta\oplus 1}
are identically distributed,
it holds that

\Pr[f[j+1]=\beta]=\Pr[f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1]=\frac{1}{2}
.
If
b=0
and
f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1
,
\mathpzc{B}
simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]

\displaystyle=\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\wedge f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1\mid b=0]

(260)

\displaystyle=\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0,f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1]\cdot\Pr[f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1]

(261)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}=1].

(262)

If
b=1
and
f[j+1]=\beta\oplus 1
,
\mathpzc{B}
simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j+1}
.
Similarly, we have

\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\frac{1}{2}\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j+1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{j}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{j+1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore

\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}
is non-negligible, which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma
.
∎

Appendix E
Garbling Scheme with Certified Everlasting Deletion

In
Section

E.1
, we define a garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion.
In
Section

E.2
, we construct a certified everlasting secure garbling scheme from a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme.

E.1
Definition

We define a garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion below. An important difference from a standard classical garbling scheme is that the garbled circuit
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
(i.e., an output of
\mathsf{Grbl}
)
is a quantum state.

Definition E.1 (Garbling Scheme with Certified Everlasting Deletion (Syntax)).

Let
\lambda
be a security parameter and
p,q,r
and
s
be polynomials.
Let
\mathcal{C}_{n}
be a family of circuits that take
n
-bit inputs.
A garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion is a tuple of algorithms
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{Garble},\mathpzc{Eval},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

with label space
\mathcal{L}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}
, garbled circuit space
\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes q(\lambda)}
,
verification key space
\mathcal{VK}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{r(\lambda)}
and deletion certificate space
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{\otimes s(\lambda)}
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
:

The sampling algorithm takes a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
as input, and outputs
2n
labels
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
with
L_{i,\alpha}\in\mathcal{L}
for each
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
.

\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})\rightarrow(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})
:

The garbling algorithm takes
1^{\lambda}
, a circuit
C\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and
2n
labels
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}
as input, and outputs a garbled circuit
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}\in\mathcal{C}
and a verification key
\mathsf{vk}\in\mathcal{VK}
.

\mathpzc{Eval}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})\rightarrow y
:

The evaluation algorithm takes
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
and
n
labels
\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]}
where
x_{i}\in\{0,1\}
as input,
and outputs
y
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}})\rightarrow\mathsf{cert}
:

The deletion algorithm takes
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
as input, and outputs a certificate
\mathsf{cert}\in\mathcal{D}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})\rightarrow\top
or
\bot
:

The verification algorithm takes
\mathsf{vk}
and
\mathsf{cert}
as input, and outputs
\top
or
\bot
.

We require that a garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion satisfies correctness defined below.

Definition E.2 (Correctness for Garbling Scheme with Certified Everlasting Deletion).

There are two types of correctness, namely, evaluation correctness and verification correctness.

Evaluation Correctness:

There exists a negligible function
{\mathsf{negl}}
such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,
C\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[y\neq C(x)\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})\\
y\leftarrow\mathpzc{Eval}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification Correctness:

There exists a negligible function
{\mathsf{negl}}
such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})=\bot\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})\\
\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Del}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Minimum requirements for correctness are evaluation correctness and verification correctness.
However, we also require verification correctness with QOTP in this work because we need it for the construction of FE in
Section

4.3
.

Definition E.3 (Verification Correctness with QOTP).

There exists a negligible function
{\mathsf{negl}}
and a PPT algorithm
\mathsf{Recover}
such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert}^{*})=\bot\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})\\
a,b\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{q(\lambda)}\\
\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Del}(Z^{b}X^{a}\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}X^{a}Z^{b})\\
\mathsf{cert}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Recover}(a,b,\widetilde{\mathsf{cert}})\end{array}\right]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

As security, we consider two definitions,

Definition

E.4

and

Definition

E.5
given below.
The former is just the standard selective security
and the latter is the certified everlasting security
that we newly define in this paper.
Roughly, the everlasting security guarantees that any QPT adversary with the garbled circuit
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
and the labels
\{L_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]}
cannot obtain any information
beyond
C(x)
even if it becomes computationally unbounded after it issues a valid certificate.

Definition E.4 (Selective Security for Garbling Scheme with Certified Everlasting Deletion).

Let
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{Garble},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Eval},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion.
We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{gbl}}(1^{\lambda},b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
. Let
\mathpzc{Sim}
be a QPT algorithm.

1.

\mathpzc{A}
sends a circuit
C\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and an input
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
to the challenger.

2.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

3.

If
b=0
, the challenger computes
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
, and returns
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.
If
b=1
, the challenger computes
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|C|},C(x),\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
,
and returns
(\widetilde{C},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
. The experiment outputs
b^{\prime}
.

We say that
\Sigma
is selective secure if there exists a QPT simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}
such that for any QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{gbl}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{sel}{gbl}(1^\secp,0)=1]-\Pr[\expb{\Sigma,\qA}{sel}{gbl}(1^\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(274)

Definition E.5 (Selective Certified Everlasting Security for Garbling Scheme).

Let
\Sigma=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{Garble},\mathpzc{Eval},\mathpzc{Del},\allowbreak\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a garbling scheme with certified everlasting deletion.
We consider the following security experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\Sigma}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{gbl}}(1^{\lambda},b)
against a QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and an unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
. Let
\mathpzc{Sim}
be a QPT algorithm.

1.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends a circuit
C\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and an input
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
to the challenger.

2.

The challenger computes
\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

3.

If
b=0
, the challenger computes
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\alpha}\}_{i\in[n],\alpha\in\{0,1\}})
, and returns
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.
If
b=1
, the challenger computes
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Sim}(1^{\lambda},1^{|C|},C(x),\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
,
and returns
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\mathsf{cert}
to the challenger, and sends the internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

5.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
.
If the output is
\bot
, then the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\top
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

7.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

We say that
\Sigma
is selective certified everlasting secure if there exists a QPT simulator
\mathpzc{Sim}
such that for any QPT
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{gbl}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expd{\Sigma,\qA}{cert}{ever}{sel}{gbl}(1^\secp,0)=1]-\Pr[\expd{\Sigma,\qA}{cert}{ever}{sel}{gbl}(1^\secp,1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(275)

E.2
Construction

In this section, we construct a certified everlasting secure garbling scheme from a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme (
Definition

C.1
).
Our construction is similar to Yao’s construction of a standard garbling scheme
[
Yao86
]
, but there are two important differences.
First, we use a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme instead of a standard SKE scheme.
Second, we use XOR secret sharing, although
[
Yao86
]
used double encryption. The reason why we cannot use double encryption is
that our certified everlasting SKE scheme has quantum ciphertext and classical plaintext.

Before introducing our construction, let us quickly review notations for circuits.
Let
C
be a boolean circuit.
A boolean circuit
C
consists of gates,
\mathsf{gate}_{1},\mathsf{gate}_{2},\cdots,\mathsf{gate}_{q}
,
where
q
is the number of gates in the circuit.
Here,
\mathsf{gate}_{i}\coloneqq(g,w_{a},w_{b},w_{c})
, where
g:\{0,1\}^{2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
is a function,
w_{a}
,
w_{b}
are the incoming wires, and
w_{c}
is the outgoing wire.
(The number of outgoing wires is not necessarily one. There can be many outgoing wires, but we use the same label
w_{c}
for all outgoing wires.)
We say
C
is leveled if each gate has an associated level and any gate at level
\ell
has incoming wires only from gates at level
\ell-1
and outgoing wires only to gates at level
\ell+1
.
Let
\mathsf{out}_{1},\mathsf{out}_{2},\cdots,\mathsf{out}_{m}
be the
m
output wires.
For any
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,
C(x)
is the output of the circuit
C
on input
x
.
We consider that
\mathsf{gate_{1}},\mathsf{gate_{2}},\cdots,\mathsf{gate}_{q}
are arranged in the ascending order of the level.

Our certified secure everlasting garbling scheme.

We construct a certified everlasting secure garbling scheme
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{Garble},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Eval},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})

from a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme

\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}=\mathsf{SKE}.(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
(
Definition

C.1
).
Let
\mathcal{K}
be the key space of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
.
Let
C
be a leveled boolean circuit.
Let
n,m,q
, and
p
be the input size,
the output size,
the number of gates,
and
the total number of wires
of
C
,
respectively.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

For each
i\in[n]
and
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
, generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{i}^{\sigma}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\{L_{i,\sigma}\}_{i\in[n],\sigma\in\{0,1\}}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{i}^{\sigma}\}_{i\in[n],\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
.

\mathpzc{Garble}(1^{\lambda},C,\{L_{i,\sigma}\}_{i\in[n],\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
:

•

For each
i\in\{n+1,\cdots,p\}
and
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
, generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{i}^{\sigma}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

For each
i\in[q]
,
compute

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{GateGrbl}(\mathsf{gate}_{i},\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}),

(276)

where
\mathsf{gate}_{i}=(g,w_{a},w_{b},w_{c})
and
\mathpzc{GateGrbl}
is described in Fig
7
.

•

For each
i\in[m]
,
set
\widetilde{d}_{i}\coloneqq[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}}^{0},0),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}}^{1},1)]
.

•

Output
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}\coloneqq(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d}_{i}\}_{i\in[m]})

and

\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
.

\mathsf{Eval}(\widetilde{C},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
:

•

Parse
\widetilde{C}=(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d}_{i}\}_{i\in[m]})
and
\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]}=\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
.

•

For each
i\in[q]
,
compute
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{c}\leftarrow\mathpzc{GateEval}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{a},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{b})
in the ascending order of the level, where
\mathpzc{GateEval}
is described in Fig
8
.
If
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{c}=\bot
, output
\bot
and abort.

•

For each
i\in[m]
, set
y[i]=\sigma
if
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}}=\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}}^{\sigma}
. Otherwise, set
y[i]=\bot
, and abort.

•

Output
y\coloneqq y[1]||y[2]||\cdots||y[m]
.

\mathsf{Del}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}})
:

•

Parse
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}=(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d}_{i}\}_{i\in[m]})
.

•

For each
i\in[q]
, compute
\mathsf{cert}_{i}\leftarrow\mathpzc{GateDel}(\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i})
, where
\mathpzc{GateDel}
is described in Fig
9
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse

\mathsf{vk}=\{\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}

and
\mathsf{cert}=\{\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
.

•

For each
i\in[q]
,
compute
\bot/\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GateVrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
