\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1},y),

(284)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{v(\beta)},y\oplus y_{b}),

(285)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1},z),

(286)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{v(\beta)\oplus 1},z\oplus z_{b}),

(287)

and sends

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1})

(288)

to
\mathpzc{B}
.

8.

\mathpzc{B}
samples
\gamma_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{S}_{4}
.

\mathpzc{B}
sets
\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{j}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\alpha}^{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{\beta}^{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}}\}_{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}\in\{0,1\}}
in the permutated order of
\gamma_{j}
.

9.

For each
i\in\{j+1,j+2,\cdots,q\}
,
\mathpzc{B}
computes
(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{GateGrbl}(\mathsf{gate}_{i},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
, where
\mathpzc{B}
calls the encryption query of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
if
\mathpzc{B}
needs to use
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1}
or
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{v(\beta)\oplus 1}
to run
(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{GateGrbl}(\mathsf{gate}_{i},\allowbreak\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
.

10.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\widetilde{d_{i}}\coloneqq[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{0}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}},0),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}^{1}_{\mathsf{out}_{i}},1)]
for
i\in[m]
,
sets
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}\coloneqq(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]})
,
and sends
(\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}},\{L_{i,x_{i}}\}_{i\in[n]})
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

11.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{cert}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
to
\mathpzc{B}
and the internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
, respectively.

12.

\mathpzc{B}
re-sorts
\mathsf{cert}_{j}=\{\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}}\}_{\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta}\in\{0,1\}}
according to
\gamma_{j}
.

\mathpzc{B}
sends

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha)\oplus 1,v(\beta)\oplus 1})

(289)

to the challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
and receives
\bot
or
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}v(\alpha)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}v(\beta)\oplus 1})
from the challenger.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})

and
\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{GateVrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
for each
i\in\{1,2,\cdots,j-1,j+1,j+2,\cdots,q\}
,
where
\mathsf{GateVrfy}
is described in Fig
10
.
If
\mathpzc{B}
receives
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}v(\alpha)\oplus 1},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}v(\beta)\oplus 1})
from the challenger,

\top\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\alpha}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
,

\top\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{cert}_{\beta}^{v(\alpha),v(\beta)})
,
and
\top\leftarrow\mathsf{GateVrfy}(\mathsf{cert}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i})
for all

i\in\{1,2,\cdots,j-1,j+1,j+2,\cdots,q\}
, then
\mathpzc{B}
sends
\top
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
, and aborts.

13.

\mathpzc{B}
outputs the output of
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j-1}=1]
and
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}=1]
.
Therefore, if for an adversary
\mathpzc{A}
,

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j-1}=1]-\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}=1]}

is non-negligible, then

\left|\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)=1]-\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}},\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}\allowbreak(\lambda,1)=1]\right|

(290)

is non-negligible.
From
Lemma

E.10
, it contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

E.9
.

To show
Proposition

E.9
, it is sufficient to prove that the probability distribution of
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
in
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
is statistically identical to
that of
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
.

First, let us remind the difference between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
and
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
.
In both experiments ,
\widetilde{\mathpzc{C}}
consists of
\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
and
\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]}
.
On the other hand the contents of
\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
and
\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]}
are different in each experiments.
In
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
,

\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}
consists of
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})

where

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}),

(291)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma_{b}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}\oplus\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}),

(292)

and

\widetilde{d_{i}}
is

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{0},0),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{1},1)].

(293)

In
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{select}}(1^{\lambda},1)
,

\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}
consists of
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})

where

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{a}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{a}^{\sigma_{a}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}),

(294)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{ct}_{b}^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{b}^{\sigma_{b}},p^{\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b}}_{c}\oplus\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}),

(295)

and

\widetilde{d_{i}}
is

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{0},C(x)_{i}),(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{\mathsf{out_{i}}}^{1},C(x)_{i}\oplus 1)].

(296)

The resulting distribution of
(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]})
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}
is statistically identical to
the resulting distribution of
(\{\widetilde{\mathpzc{g}}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\widetilde{d_{i}}\}_{i\in[m]})
in
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma_{\mathsf{cegc}},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}{select}(1^{\lambda},1)
.
This is because, at any level that is not output, the keys
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{1}
are used completely identically in the subsequent level so there is no difference
between always encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}
and
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}
.
At the output level, there is no difference between encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}
and giving the real mapping
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{v(c)}\rightarrow v(c)

or encrypting
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}
and giving the programming mapping
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{sk}_{c}^{0}\rightarrow C(x)_{i}
, which completes the proof.
∎

We use the following lemma for the proof of
Proposition

E.8
.
The proof is shown with the standard hybrid argument. It is also easy to see that a similar lemma holds for IND-CPA security.

Lemma E.10.

Let
\Sigma\coloneqq(\mathsf{KeyGen},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
be a certified everlasting secure SKE scheme.
Let us consider the following security experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,b)
against
\mathpzc{A}
consisting of any QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

1.

The challenger generates
(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
can call encryption queries. More formally, it can do the followings:
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{SK}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to the challenger.

•

If
\beta=0
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and

(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
can repeat this process polynomially many times.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
generates
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
and chooses two triples of messages
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})\in\mathcal{M}^{3}
and
(x_{1},y_{1},z_{1})\in\mathcal{M}^{3}
, and sends
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1},\{x_{\sigma},y_{\sigma},z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
to the challenger.

4.

The challenger generates
(x,y,z)\leftarrow\mathcal{M}^{3}
.
The challenger computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{0})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{0},x),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},x\oplus x_{b})

(297)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{0})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},y),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{1},y\oplus y_{b})

(298)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{0})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},z),\,\,\,(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},z\oplus z_{b})

(299)

and sends
\{\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
can call encryption queries. More formally, it can do the followings:

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{SK}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to the challenger.

•

If
\beta=0
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
, the challenger generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and

(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
can repeat this process polynomially many times.

6.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to the challenger, and sends the internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

7.

The challenger computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma})
,
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma})
and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{\sigma},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{\sigma})
for each
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
. If all results are
\top
, then the challenger outputs
\top
, and sends
\{\mathsf{sk}^{\prime\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.
Otherwise, the challenger outputs
\bot
, and sends
\bot
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

8.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

9.

If the challenger outputs
\top
, then the output of the experiment is
b^{\prime}
. Otherwise, the output of the experiment is
\bot
.

If the
\Sigma
satisfies the certified everlasting IND-CPA security,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[ \expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1] - \Pr[ \expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 1)=1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

(300)

for any QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
and any unbounded adversary
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.10
.

We define the following hybrid experiment.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)
except that the challenger encrypts
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{1})
instead of encrypting
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is identical to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except that the challenger encrypts
(x_{0},y_{1},z_{1})
instead of encrypting
(x_{0},y_{0},z_{1})
.

Lemma

E.10
easily follows from the following
Propositions

E.11
,
E.12
and
E.13
(whose proof is given later.).
∎

Proposition E.11.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(301)

Proposition E.12.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(302)

Proposition E.13.

If
\Sigma
is certified everlasting IND-CPA secure, it holds that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hyb_{2}=1]-\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 1)=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(303)

Proof of
Proposition

E.11
.

We assume that

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}(1)=1]}

is non-negligible, and construct an adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that breaks the security experiment of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
.
This contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma
.
Let us describe how
\mathpzc{B}
works.

1.

The challenger of
\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{B}}^{\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cpa}}(\lambda,b)
generates
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
, and
\mathpzc{B}
generates
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1}\leftarrow\mathsf{KeyGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{K}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

•

If
\beta=0
,
\mathpzc{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, sends
m^{*}
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{m}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{m}^{0})
from the challenger, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
,
\mathpzc{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathpzc{B}
repeats this process when
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
is sent from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

3.

\mathpzc{B}
receives
(\mathsf{sk}^{0},\mathsf{sk}^{1},\{x_{\sigma},y_{\sigma},z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}})
from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

4.

\mathpzc{B}
generates
(x,y,z)\leftarrow\mathcal{M}^{3}
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes

\displaystyle(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{0})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{0},x),(\mathsf{vk}_{x}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},x\oplus x_{0}),

(304)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{1},y\oplus y_{0}),

(305)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{1})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},z\oplus z_{0}).

(306)

5.

\mathpzc{B}
sets
m_{0}\coloneqq z
and
m_{1}\coloneqq z\oplus z_{0}\oplus z_{1}
.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
(m_{0},m_{1})
to the challenger.

6.

The challenger computes
(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{0})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0},m_{b})
, and sends
\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{0}
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

7.

\mathpzc{B}
sends an encryption query
y
to the challenger, and receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{y}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{0})
.

8.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathpzc{ct}_{x}^{\sigma},\mathpzc{ct}_{y}^{\sigma},\mathpzc{ct}_{z}^{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

9.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
chooses
\beta\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{sk}\in\mathcal{K}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
to
\mathpzc{B}
.

•

If
\beta=0
,
\mathpzc{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, sends
m^{*}
to the challenger, receives
(\mathsf{vk}_{m}^{0},\mathpzc{ct}_{m}^{0})
from the challenger, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m\oplus m^{*})
, and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

•

If
\beta=1
,
\mathpzc{B}
generates
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
, computes
(\mathsf{vk}^{1}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{1}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 1},m\oplus m^{*})
and
(\mathsf{vk}^{0}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{0}_{m})\leftarrow\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{sk},m^{*})
and sends
\{\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{m},\mathpzc{ct}^{\sigma}_{m}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

\mathpzc{B}
repeats this process when
(\beta,\mathsf{sk},m)
is sent from
\mathpzc{A}_{1}
.

10.

\mathpzc{A}_{1}
sends
\{\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{x},\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{y},\mathsf{cert}^{\sigma}_{z}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{B}
, and sends the internal state to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

11.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{0}
to the challenger, and receives
\mathsf{sk}^{\prime 0}
or
\bot
from the challenger.
If
\mathpzc{B}
receives
\bot
, it outputs
\bot
and aborts.

12.

\mathpzc{B}
sends
\{\mathsf{sk}^{\prime\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{2}
.

13.

\mathpzc{A}_{2}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

14.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{x},\mathsf{cert}_{x}^{\sigma})
and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}^{\sigma}_{y},\mathsf{cert}_{y}^{\sigma})
for each
\sigma\in\{0,1\}
, and
\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{z}^{1},\mathsf{cert}_{z}^{1})
.
If all results are
\top
,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\bot
.

It is clear that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=0]=\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\Sigma,\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{parallel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cert}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ever}}(\lambda,0)=1]
.
Since
z
is uniformly distributed,
(z,z\oplus z_{1})
and
(z\oplus z_{0}\oplus z_{1},z\oplus z_{0})
are identically distributed.
Therefore, it holds that
\Pr[1\leftarrow\mathpzc{B}\mid b=1]=\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}=1]
.
By assumption,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\Sigma,\qA}{parallel}{cert}{ever}(\secp, 0)=1]-\Pr[\hyb_{1}=1]}
is non-negligible, and therefore

\absolutevalue{\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=0]-\Pr[1\la\qB\mid b=1]}

(307)

is non-negligible, which contradicts the certified everlasting IND-CPA security of
\Sigma_{\mathsf{cesk}}
.
∎
